Amoeba having sufficient nutriment do not strongly react on CAM. While suffering from hunger, the reaction sensitivity
to CAM increases, and with a small delay, the CAM concentration
is amplified by famished cells that, additionally by being hungry, scent CAM. By this stimulus, the amoeba enters the aggregation phase, which is characterised by all hungry amoeba coming
together, form a slug, and ends with building a fruiting body
which finally releases the spores. Theoretical and mathematical modeling of chemotaxis
dates to the works of Patlak
[
24
]
in the
1950
s and Keller and Segel
[
20
]
in the 1970s.
The Keller-Segel model describes the aggregation phase
which models the directed movement of microorganisms and cells stimulated by
a chemical produced by themselves.
For more details, we refer to the
surveys about chemotaxis by the compelling articles of Horstmann
[
16
]
, the summary of Hillen et al.
[
15
]
and the more recently
summaries of Bellomo et al.
[
2
]
and of Perthame
[
26
]
.

Aerobic bacteria often live in thin fluid layers near solid-air-water contact lines. In the absence of fluid, cells can move in a straight line or exhibit random motion, while in the presence of fluid, cells may experience hydrodynamic interactions with the surrounding fluid and may undergo fluid-mediated transport, such as diffusion, advection, or convective mixing. Fluid flow can also affect the formation and stability of chemical gradients, which are essential for chemotaxis. Here, the chemo–attractant diffuses linearly, is transported in the fluid flow direction and is consumed by the cells. On the other hand, the cell’s motion is
influenced by linear or nonlinear diffusion, transported along the fluid velocity, and partially directed in the direction of the chemoattractant
gradient. The chemotaxis system describes a biological movement in which cells (e.g., bacteria) accumulate by preferentially moving towards higher concentrations of chemotactic substances. Experiments have shown that the mechanism is a chemotactic movement of bacteria, often towards a higher concentration of oxygen, which they consume, a gravitational effect on the motion of the fluid by the heavier bacteria, and a convective transport of both cells and oxygen through the water. We refer to
[
22
,
31
]
for more details about its physical background.

We consider an initial–boundary value problem for the incompressible stochastic chemotaxis–Stokes equations with the porous-media-type diffusion in a complete probability space
\mathfrak{A}=(\Omega,{{\mathcal{F}}},{{\mathbb{F}}},{\mathbb{P}})

with filtration
{{\mathbb{F}}}=({{\mathcal{F}}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
satisfying the usual conditions i.e.,

\mathbb{P}
is complete on
(\Omega,{{\mathcal{F}}})
,
for each
t\geq 0
,
{{\mathcal{F}}}_{t}
contains all
({{\mathcal{F}}},\mathbb{P})
-null sets, and
the filtration
({{\mathcal{F}}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}
is right-continuous.
Here, the underlying spatial domain
{{\mathcal{O}}}
is a bounded domain of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Let
W_{1}
,
W_{2}
and
W_{3}
be three independent cylindrical Wiener processes on
L^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
defined over the probability space

\mathfrak{A}
by

\displaystyle W_{j}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\varphi_{k}\,\mathbf{w}^{j}_{k}(t),\quad t\in[0,T],\,\quad j=1,2,3,

(3.1)

where
\{\mathbf{w}^{j}_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
,
j=1,2,3
, are two mutually independent families of i.i.d. standard scalar Wiener processes and
\{\varphi_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
the eigenfunction of the Laplace operator
(-\Delta)
on
L^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
corresponding to the associated eigenvalues
\{\lambda_{k}:k\in\mathbb{N}\}
. We investigate the following coupled system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}d{n}+\delta_{n}\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla ndt&=&\left[r_{n}\Delta|n|^{q-1}n+\theta n-\chi\mbox{div}(n\nabla c)\right]\,dt+g_{\gamma_{1}}(n)\circ dW_{1},\\
d{c}+\delta_{c}\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla cdt&=&\left[r_{c}\Delta c-\zeta c+\beta n\right]\,dt+g_{\gamma_{2}}(c)\circ dW_{2},\\
d\mathbf{u}+\nabla Pdt&=&\left[r_{\mathbf{u}}\Delta\mathbf{u}+n\star\Phi\right]dt+\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}\,dW_{3}\\
\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}&=&0\end{array}\right.

with boundary and initial condition conditions

\begin{split}&\nabla c\cdot\nu=0,\qquad\nabla n\cdot\nu=0,\qquad\mathbf{u}=0,\quad\text{ on }\partial\mathcal{O},\\
&c(0)=c_{0},\qquad n(0)=n_{0},\qquad\mathbf{u}(0)=\mathbf{u}_{0},\end{split}

(3.6)

on a filtered probability space
\mathfrak{A}
where the noise intensities
\gamma_{j}
,
j=1,2,3,
are given positive real numbers and the linear diffusion coefficients
g_{\gamma_{1}}
,
g_{\gamma_{2}}
and
\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}
are defined respectively by

\displaystyle g_{\gamma_{j}}(\psi)h

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\psi\cdot(-\Delta)^{-\gamma_{j}/2}h=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}^{-\gamma_{j}/2}u\cdot\varphi_{k}\langle h,\varphi_{k}\rangle,\quad\psi\in L^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}}),\ h\in H,\ \ j=1,2,

(3.7)

and

\displaystyle\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}h

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(-\Delta)^{-\gamma_{3}/2}h=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}^{-\gamma_{3}/2}\varphi_{k}\langle h,\varphi_{k}\rangle,\quad h\in H.

(3.8)

In system (
3
), the unknowns are the cell density
n
, the concentration of the chemical signal
c
, the pressure
P
and velocity field
\mathbf{u}
a over the domain
\mathcal{O}
.
The positive terms
r_{u}
and
r_{v}
are the diffusivity of the cells and chemo-attractant respectively, the positive value
\chi
is the chemotactic sensitivity,
and the positive constant
\zeta
is the so-called damping constant. The given function
\Phi=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
represents the kernel of gravitational potential. We then recall that the system (
1.1
) is equivalent to the following Itô system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}d{n}+\delta_{n}\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla ndt&=&\left[r_{n}\Delta|n|^{q-1}n+\theta n-\chi\mbox{div}(n\nabla c)\right]\,dt+g_{\gamma_{1}}(n)dW_{1},\\
d{c}+\delta_{c}\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla cdt&=&\left[r_{c}\Delta c-\alpha c+\beta n\right]\,dt+g_{\gamma_{2}}(c)dW_{2},\\
d\mathbf{u}+\nabla Pdt&=&\left[r_{\mathbf{u}}\Delta\mathbf{u}+n\star\Phi\right]dt+\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}\,dW_{3},\\
\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}&=&0,\end{array}\right.

where
\alpha=\zeta-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}_{2}
and
\theta=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\lambda_{k}^{-\gamma_{1}}
.

We now introduce the following spaces

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}=\left\{\mathbf{u}\in\mathcal{C}_{c}^{\infty}(\mathcal{O},\mathbb{R}^{2})\,\,\text{such that}\,\,\nabla\cdot\mathbf{u}=0\right\},

\displaystyle\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O})=\,\,\text{closure of $\mathcal{V}$ in }\,\,(L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))^{2},

\displaystyle\mathbf{H}=\,\,\text{closure of $\mathcal{V}$ in }\,\,(H_{0}^{1}(\mathcal{O}))^{2},\qquad\text{ and }\qquad\mathbf{V}=\mathbf{H}\cap(H^{2}(\mathcal{O}))^{2}.

We endow
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O})
with the scalar product and norm of
(L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))^{2}
. As
usual we equip the space
\mathbf{H}
with the the scalar product

(\nabla\mathbf{u},\nabla\mathbf{v})_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}
which, owing to the Poincaré inequality, is equivalent to the

(H^{1}(\mathcal{O}))^{2}
-scalar product. Let
\Pi:(L^{2}(\mathcal{O}))^{2}\rightarrow\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O})
be the Helmholtz-Leray projection. We denote by

A=-\Pi\Delta

the
Stokes operator with domain
D(A)=\mathbf{V}
.

In case of our setting, we define the notion of martingale solution to our problem as follows.

Definition 3.1
.

Fix
T>0
. A martingale solution to the problem
(
3
) is given by the tuple

\left(\Omega,{{\mathcal{F}}},\mathbb{P},{\mathbb{F}},(W_{1},W_{2},W_{3}),n,c,\mathbf{u}\right),

such that

•

\mathfrak{A}:=(\Omega,{{\mathcal{F}}},{\mathbb{F}},\mathbb{P})
is a complete filtered
probability space with a filtration
{\mathbb{F}}=\{{{\mathcal{F}}}_{t}:t\in[0,T]\}
satisfying the usual conditions,

•

W_{1}
,
W_{2}
and
W_{3}
are three cylindrical Wiener processes on
H
over the probability space

\mathfrak{A}
, and

•

n:[0,T]\times\Omega\rightarrow H^{-1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
, and
c:[0,T]\times\Omega\rightarrow L^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
, and
\mathbf{u}:[0,T]\times\Omega\rightarrow{\bf H}

are
{{\mathbb{F}}}
–progressively measurable processes such that
{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s.

(n,c,\mathbf{u})\in C([0,T];H^{-1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}}))\times C([0,T];L^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}}))\times C([0,T];\mathbf{L}^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})),

and satisfy for all
t\in[0,T]
the following stochastic integral equations

{\mathbb{P}}
–a.s.,

\displaystyle n(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{u}(s)\cdot\nabla n(s)\,ds

\displaystyle=n_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(r_{u}\Delta\left|n(s)\right|^{q-1}n(s)+\theta n(s)-\chi\mbox{div}(n(s)\nabla c(s))\,ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{t}g_{\gamma_{1}}(n(s))dW_{1}(s),

(3.13)

c(t)+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{u}(s)\cdot\nabla c(s)\,ds=c_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(r_{c}\Delta c(s)-\alpha c(s)+\beta n(s))\,ds+\int_{0}^{t}g_{\gamma_{2}}(c(s))dW_{2}(s),

(3.14)

and

\mathbf{u}(t)-r_{\mathbf{u}}\int_{0}^{t}A\mathbf{u}(s)ds=\int_{0}^{t}n(s)\star\Phi ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}dW_{3}(s),

(3.15)

where the equations (
•
‣
3.1
), (
3.14
) and (
3.15
) hold in
H^{-1}_{2}(\mathcal{O})
,
L^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
and
\mathbf{L}^{2}(\mathcal{O})
respectively.

In what follows, we gather assumptions that we will need for the proof of the main result of this section.

Assumption 3.2
.

We will assume that

(A1):

The noise intensity
\gamma_{j}
,
j=1,2,3
, satisfy
\gamma_{1}>2
,
\gamma_{2}>2
and
\gamma_{3}>1
.

(A2):

q>4
and the kernel of gravitational potential
\Phi=(\phi_{1},\phi_{2})
where
\phi_{i}=e^{-\delta_{i}\Delta}
, with some fixed real numbers
\delta_{i}>0
,
i=1,2
.

(A3):

The initial condition
(n_{0},c_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0})
is a
H^{-1}_{2}(\mathcal{O})\times H^{1}_{2}(\mathcal{O})\times{\bf H}
-valued
{{\mathcal{F}}}_{0}
-measurable random variable satisfying

\mathbb{E}|n_{0}|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}<\infty,\ \mathbb{E}\left|c_{0}\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}<\infty,\ \mathbb{E}\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}<\infty,

(3.16)

where
r^{\ast}
and
m^{\ast}
are real numbers such that

\displaystyle q\leq r^{*}<q+1\quad\mbox{and}\quad m^{*}\geq 2q+2.

Remark 3.3
.

Assumption (A1) is needed to insure that
|\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}|_{L_{HS}(L^{2},{\bf H})}<\infty
and also the existence of a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|g_{\gamma_{1}}(\psi)|_{L_{HS}(L^{2},H^{-1}_{2})}^{2}\leq C|\psi|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2},\quad\psi\in L^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}}),

(3.17)

\displaystyle|g_{\gamma_{2}}(\psi)|_{L_{HS}(L^{2},L^{2})}^{2}\leq C|\psi|^{2}_{L^{2}},\quad\psi\in L^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}}),

(3.18)

where
L_{HS}(L^{2},E)
denote the space of Hilbert-Schmit operators defined from
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
into
E
, with
E
either
{\bf H}
,
L^{2}(\mathcal{O})
or
H^{-1}_{2}(\mathcal{O})
.
We refer to Appendix
A
for more explanation.

The main result of this section is stated in the following theorem.

Theorem 3.4
.

Let Assumption
3.2
holds. Then
the system (
3
)-(
3.6
) has a martingale solution in the sense of Definition
3.1
.
In addition, for all
p\in[1,\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}]
there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2p}_{\bf H}+\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c_{k}(s)\right|^{2p}_{L^{2}}+\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}|n_{\kappa}(s)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2p}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{p}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds\right)^{p}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf V}ds\right)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq e^{CT}\left(1+\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{2p}_{\bf H}+\mathbb{E}\left|c_{0}\right|^{2p}_{H_{2}^{1}}+\mathbb{E}|n_{0}|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2p}\right).

(3.19)

Proof.

The proof will use the Meta Theorem
2.2
. Here, we will only prove that the assumptions (
a
) to (
f
) and the bound in (
2.21
) are satisfied.

The proof consists of three steps. In the first step, we will define the underlying spaces and the operator.
In the second step, we will
show that the assumptions (
a
) to (
f
) and the bound in
2.21
of Theorem
2.2
are satisfied.
In the third step, we will apply the Theorem
2.2
.
The estimates regarded in (
2.21
) will give the regularity property of the solution.

Step (I)

In the first step, we will define the underlying spaces, the argument of the cut-off function, the operator, and the subspace on which the operator acts.
Let us denote by
\mathbb{X}
the space

\mathbb{X}=L^{m^{\ast}}(0,T;H^{-s^{**}}_{r^{*}}({{\mathcal{O}}})),\text{ where }s^{**}=\frac{2}{q+1},

and by
{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})
the space being defined by

\displaystyle{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\Big\{\xi:{{\mathcal{O}}}\times[0,T]\times\Omega\to{\mathbb{R}},\,\,

\displaystyle\quad\mbox{ $\xi$ is $({{\mathcal{F}}}_{t})_{t\geq 0}$-progressively measurable and }\,\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{m^{\ast}}<\infty\Big\}.

Let us define the arguments of the cut-off function by

\displaystyle h_{1}(\mathbf{u},t)=h(\mathbf{u},t)=\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}|\mathbf{u}(s)|_{{\bf H}}.

The cut-off function is given by the functions
\phi_{\kappa}
, defined by the relation (
2.10
)

\displaystyle\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u},t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\phi_{\kappa}(h(\mathbf{u},t)),\quad t\in[0,T].

Define for some real number
R>0
, the set
{\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa}^{{\mathfrak{A}}}={\mathcal{X}}_{\kappa}^{\mathfrak{A}}(R)
, where

{\mathcal{X}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}(R):=\left\{\xi\in{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X}):\ \mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|\xi(s)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\xi(s)|_{L^{q+1}}^{q+1}\,ds\right)^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq R\right\}.

We recall that since
m^{\ast}>q+1
,
2<r^{\ast}<q+1
and
s^{\ast\ast}\in(0,1)
, we have
\frac{m^{\ast}}{r^{\ast}}>1
. By Proposition
B.1
, we can derive that for all
R>0
, the set
{\mathcal{X}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}(R)
is a bounded convex set in
{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})
. In fact, for
\xi\in{\mathcal{X}}^{\kappa}_{{\mathfrak{A}}}(R)
it follows by Proposition
B.1
, that we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\xi\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{m^{\ast}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|\xi(s)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\xi(s)|_{L^{q+1}}^{q+1}\,ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}|\xi(s)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\xi(s)|_{L^{q+1}}^{q+1}\,ds\right)^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step (II)

We will show that the framework satisfies the assumptions of Theorem
2.2
. For this aim, let us consider for any integer
\kappa\geq 1
, the mapping

{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{\mathfrak{A}},\mathbb{W}}^{\kappa}:{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})\to{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X}),

defined by for
\xi\in{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})
,
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{\mathfrak{A}},\mathbb{W}}^{\kappa}(\xi):=n_{\kappa}
, where the triple
(n_{\kappa},c_{\kappa},\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})
solves the following system.

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}d{n}_{\kappa}(t)&=&\left(r_{n}\Delta|n_{\kappa}(t)|^{q-1}n_{\kappa}(t)+\theta\xi(t)-\chi\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t))\right)\,dt\\
&&{}+\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla\xi(t)\,dt+g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}(t))\,dW_{1}(t),\\
d{c_{\kappa}}(t)&=&\left(r_{c}\Delta c_{\kappa}(t)-\alpha c_{\kappa}(t)+\beta\xi(t)\right)\,dt\\
&&{}+\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa},t)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\,dt+g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(t))\,dW_{2}(t),\\
d\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)&=&r_{\mathbf{u}}A\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)dt+\xi(t)\star\Phi dt+\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}dW_{3}(t),\end{array}\right.

with initial condition
n_{\kappa}(0)=n_{0}
,
c_{\kappa}(0)=c_{0}
, and
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(0)=\mathbf{u}_{0}
.

Remark 3.5
.

Note, that
c_{\kappa}
and
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}
does not depend on
n_{\kappa}
but on
\xi
. In particular, the system (
2
) is split.

Verification of conditions (
a
) and (
b
) of Theorem
2.2
:
Here, we will show that the mapping
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{\mathfrak{A}},\mathbb{W}}^{\kappa}
is well defined and maps element of
\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}(R)
into itself for a certain
R>0
. In particular, to verify assumption (
a
), we will show that for any
R>0
, any
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, any initial conditions
(n_{0},c_{0},\mathbf{u}_{0})
satisfying estimates (
3.16
)
and any
\xi\in{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})\cap\mathcal{X}_{\kappa}({\mathfrak{A}})
, there exists a triple of processes
(n_{\kappa},c_{\kappa},\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})
solving system
(
2
). By the a priori estimates, which we will prove in the following claim, condition (
b
) of Theorem
2.2
follows.

Claim 3.6
.

Let Assumption
3.2
be satisfied and let
R>0
be arbitrary. For any
\xi\in{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})\cap\mathcal{X}(R)
, there exists a unique triple of processes
(n_{\kappa},c_{\kappa},\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})
solving system (
2
) such that
n_{\kappa}\in{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})
. In addition, for any
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists some constant
C=C(\kappa,m^{*},r^{*},T,q)
such that the following estimates are satisfied:

\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+r_{u}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf V}ds\right)^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}}+C,

(3.26)

\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|c_{k}(t)\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{1}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left|c_{0}\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}},

(3.27)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+(2r_{n})^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

(3.28)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}|n_{0}|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+C\mathbb{E}\left|c_{0}\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C+CR^{\frac{2}{q+1}}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}}.

where
C_{1}
is a constant to be given below.

From the estimates in Claim
3.6
follows assumption (
b
) of Theorem
2.2
.
To see this, let us for the time being assume that Claim
3.6
is true. Then, from the estimate (
3.28
), for all
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
and for all
R>0

such that

\displaystyle R

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}|n_{0}|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+C\mathbb{E}\left|c_{0}\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C+CR^{\frac{2}{q+1}}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}},

we know that for any
\xi\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}(R)
we have
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathfrak{A}}^{\kappa}(\xi)\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}(R)
(since
\frac{2}{q+1}<1
and
\frac{4}{q+1}<1
).
In particular, the operator
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathfrak{A}}^{\kappa}
maps element of
\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}(R)
into itself.

Proof of Claim
3.6
.

Let us fix
R>0
and
\xi\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}(R)
.
We will start with the last estimate and will end with the first estimate.
In the first step we consider the system

\displaystyle d\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)-r_{\mathbf{u}}A\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)dt=\xi(t)\star\Phi dt+\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}\,dW_{3}(t),

(3.29)

with initial condition
\mathbf{u}(0)=\mathbf{u}_{0}
.
Here, the existence and uniqueness of a solution are given by standard methods; see, e.g.,
[
28
, Section 13.11.1]
.

By the Itô formula to the process
t\mapsto\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{\bf H}
, we infer that

\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{2}_{\bf H}+2r_{u}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf V}ds=\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{2}_{\bf H}+2\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla(\xi(s)\star\Phi),\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s))ds+t\left|\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H,{\bf H})}+2\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s),\nabla\sigma_{\gamma_{3}})dW_{3}(s).

We note that

\displaystyle(\nabla(\xi\star\Phi),\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})

\displaystyle\leq\left|\xi\star\Phi\right|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}\left|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}\leq C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{{\bf H}}+\left|\nabla(\xi\star\Phi)\right|^{2}_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle=C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{{\bf H}}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\nabla(\xi\star\phi_{i})|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf H}+r_{u}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf V}ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{2}_{\bf H}+2\int_{0}^{T}|\nabla(\xi(s)\star\Phi)|^{2}_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}ds+C\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{\bf H}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\qquad+T\left|\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H,{\bf H})}+2\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s),\nabla\sigma_{\gamma_{3}})dW_{3}(s)\right|.

From the point (A1) of Assumption
3.2
and some properties of semigroup theory (see, e.g.
[
25
, Chap. I]
), we know that

\displaystyle|\nabla(\xi\star\Phi)|^{2}_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}=\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\nabla(\xi\star\phi_{i})|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}|\xi\star\phi_{i}|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|e^{-\delta_{i}\Delta}\xi\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|e^{-\delta_{i}\Delta}\right|_{L(H^{-1}_{2};H^{1}_{2})}^{2}\left|\xi\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\xi\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|-\Delta e^{-\delta_{i}\Delta}\right|^{2}_{L(H^{-1}_{2};H^{-1}_{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\xi\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{C}{\delta_{i}^{1/2}}.

We then infer that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+r_{u}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf V}ds\right)^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+C(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}ds\right)^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C

\displaystyle\qquad+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{{\bf H}}ds\right)^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s),\nabla\sigma_{\gamma_{3}})\,dW_{3}(s)\right|^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}.

Since
q>3
, we know that
\frac{4}{q+1}<1
. Therefore, by the Hölder inequality and the embedding of
L^{q+1}({{\mathcal{O}}})
into
H^{-1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
, we derive that

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}ds\right)^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{\frac{8m^{*}}{(q+1)r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{4}{q+1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)R^{\frac{4}{q+1}}.

By the Burkholder-Gundy-Davis inequality and Young inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s),\nabla\sigma_{\gamma_{3}})dW_{3}(s)\right|^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf H}\left|\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H,{\bf H})}ds\right)^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}=\left|\sigma_{\gamma_{3}}\right|^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{L_{HS}(H,{\bf H})}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf H}ds\right)^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}ds\leq C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}ds+C.

In addition, the Hölder inequality gives

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf H}ds\right)^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{8{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}}_{\bf H}ds.

Combining these four last inequalities we arrive at

\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+r_{u}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{\bf V}ds\right)^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}}+C+C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}ds.

Applying the Gronwall Lemma, we obtain the inequality (
3.26
).

As next, we consider the equation

\displaystyle d{c_{\kappa}}(t)

\displaystyle=\left(r_{c}\Delta c_{\kappa}(t)-\alpha c_{\kappa}(t)+\beta\xi(t)\right)\,dt

(3.30)

\displaystyle{}+\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u},t)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\,dt+\ g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(t))dW_{2}(t),\quad c_{\kappa}(0)=c_{0}.

To show existence and uniqueness of a solution, we will verify that the assumptions (H1), (H2
′
), (H3), and (H4
′
) of Theorem 5.1.3 in
[
23
, p. 125]
are satisfied. For this aim, let us consider the Gelfand triple

H^{1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})\subset L^{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})\subset H^{-1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}}),

and recall that we have

{}_{H^{-1}_{2}}\langle c,w\rangle_{H^{1}_{2}}=(c,w),\ \ \text{ for all }\ \ c\in H^{-1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})\text{ and }\ w\in H^{1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}}).

For
t\in[0,T]
, we define an operator
A_{1}(t,\cdot):H^{1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})\to H^{-1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
by

\displaystyle A_{1}(t,c):=r_{c}\Delta c-\alpha c+\beta\xi(t)+\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u},t)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla c,\quad c\in H^{1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}}),

where
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}
solves (
3.29
).

Since
A_{1}(t,\cdot)
is affine, then for all
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
and
w
in
H^{1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
, the application

\lambda\longmapsto(A_{1}(t,c_{1}+\lambda c_{2}),w)

is continue on
\mathbb{R}
. Hence the assumption (H1) is satisfy.

Now we prove the assumption (H2
′
). We fix
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
in
H^{1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
and note that

\displaystyle(A_{1}(t,c_{1})-A_{1}(t,c_{2}),c_{1}-c_{2})+\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{1}-c_{2})\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1},L^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq-(r_{c}+\alpha)\left|c_{1}-c_{2}\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}^{2}+C\left|c_{1}-c_{2}\right|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|c_{1}-c_{2}\right|_{L_{2}}^{2},

and the local monotonicity assumption (H2
′
) follows. We note that we have used the fact that after an integration-by-part

\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u},t)(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla(c_{1}-c_{2}),c_{1}-c_{2})=\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u},t)(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla(c_{1}-c_{2})^{2},1)=0.

To prove assumption (H3), we fix
c\in H^{1}({{\mathcal{O}}})
and use the fact that
\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u},t)(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla c,c)=0
to note that

\displaystyle 2(A_{1}(t,c),c)+\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c)\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1},L^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq-(r_{c}+\alpha)\left|c\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}^{2}+4\beta(\xi(t),c)+C\left|c\right|_{L_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|c\right|_{L_{2}}^{2}-(r_{c}+\alpha)\left|c\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}^{2}+C(\beta)\left|\xi(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|c\right|_{L_{2}}^{2}-\theta_{1}\left|c\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}^{\alpha_{1}}+f_{1}(t),

with
\theta_{1}=r_{c}+\alpha
and
f_{1}(t)=C(\beta)\left|\xi(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}.
Since
f_{1}\in L^{1}(\Omega\times[0,T];\mathbb{R})
, the coercivity assumption (H3) follow with
\alpha_{1}=2
.

For the assumption (H4
′
), we fix
c\in H^{1}({{\mathcal{O}}})
and note that for all
w\in H^{1}({{\mathcal{O}}})
, we have

\displaystyle(A_{1}(t,c),w)

\displaystyle=(r_{c}\Delta c-\alpha c+\beta\xi(t)+\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa},t)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla c,,w)

\displaystyle\leq-\theta_{1}\left|c\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}\left|w\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}+\beta\left|\xi\right|_{L^{2}}\left|w\right|_{L^{2}}+\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa},t)\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{\mathbf{L}^{4}}\left|\nabla c\right|_{L^{2}}\left|w\right|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq-\theta_{1}\left|c\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}\left|w\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}+\beta\left|\xi\right|_{L^{2}}\left|w\right|_{L^{2}}+\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa},t)\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{\mathbf{L}^{4}}\left|\nabla c\right|_{L^{2}}\left|w\right|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\beta\left|\xi\right|_{L^{2}}\left|w\right|_{L^{2}}+\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa},t)\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}\left|\nabla c\right|_{L^{2}}\left|w\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(\beta\left|\xi\right|_{L^{2}}+C(\kappa)\left|\nabla c\right|_{L^{2}})\left|w\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}.

This implies that for
\alpha_{1}=2
,

\displaystyle\left|A_{1}(t,c)\right|^{\frac{\alpha_{1}}{\alpha_{1}-1}}_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\beta)\left|\xi\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C(\kappa)\left|c\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq f_{1}(t)+C(\kappa)\left|c\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq(f_{1}(t)+C(\kappa)\left|c\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}})(1+\left|c\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}),

and the growth condition (H4
′
) follows.
Since the above conditions are satisfy, we invoke Theorem 5.1.3 in
[
23
]
to derive that equation (
3.30
) has a unique solution such that for all
p\geq 2
we have

\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{p}<\infty.

Now we prove the a’priori estimate for
c_{\kappa}
. For this purpose, we apply the Itô formula to the function
\phi(c_{k}):=\left|c_{k}(t)\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{1}}
to derive an estimate for

\left|c_{k}(t)\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{1}}
. Doing so, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|c_{k}(t)\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{1}}

\displaystyle=\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{1}}+2\int_{0}^{t}(r_{c}\Delta c_{\kappa}(s)-\alpha c_{\kappa}(s),c_{\kappa}(s))_{H_{2}^{1}}ds+2\beta\int_{0}^{t}(\xi(s),c_{k}(s))_{H_{2}^{1}}ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\delta_{c}\int_{0}^{t}(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa},s)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa}(s),c_{k}(s))_{H_{2}^{1}}ds

(3.31)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1};H_{2}^{1})}ds+2\int_{0}^{t}(c_{k}(s),g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))dW_{2}(s))_{H_{2}^{1}}

Using integration-by-part and the Young inequality we derive that

\displaystyle 2(r_{c}\Delta c_{\kappa}-\alpha c_{\kappa},c_{\kappa})_{H_{2}^{1}}

\displaystyle=-2(r_{c}+\alpha)\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}-2\alpha\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}-2r_{c}\left|\Delta c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq-2\alpha\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}-2r_{c}\left|\Delta c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}},

and

\displaystyle 2\beta(\xi,c_{k})_{H_{2}^{1}}

\displaystyle=2\beta(\xi,c_{k})_{L^{2}}-2\beta(\xi,\Delta c_{k})_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\xi\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon_{1}\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}+\varepsilon_{1}\left|\Delta c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\xi\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\varepsilon_{1}\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}.

In a similar way, using in addition the fact that
(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa},c_{k})_{L^{2}}=0
and the embedding of
{\bf H}
into
\mathbf{L}^{4}({{\mathcal{O}}})
, we have

\displaystyle 2(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa},c_{k})_{H_{2}^{1}}

\displaystyle=2\delta_{c}(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa},c_{k})_{L^{2}}-2\delta_{c}(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa},\Delta c_{k})_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|_{\mathbf{L}^{4}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\Delta c_{\kappa}\right|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa})\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|_{{\bf H}}\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{H_{2}^{1}}\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{H_{2}^{2}}\left|\Delta c_{\kappa}\right|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\kappa)\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{H_{2}^{1}}\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{\frac{3}{2}}_{H_{2}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\kappa)\left|c_{\kappa}\right|_{H_{2}^{1}}^{2}+\varepsilon_{1}\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{2}}.

Since
\gamma_{2}>1
, by a simple calculation, we derive that

\displaystyle\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa})\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1};H_{2}^{1})}

\displaystyle\leq C(\left|c_{\kappa}\right|_{L^{4}}+\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}\right|_{L^{4}}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C(\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}+\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{H_{2}^{1}}\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{H_{2}^{2}})\leq C\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}+\varepsilon_{1}\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{2}}.

By using these estimates we infer from the equality (
3.31
) that

\displaystyle\left|c_{k}(t)\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{1}}+2\int_{0}^{t}(\alpha\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}+r_{c}\left|\Delta c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}})ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{1}}+3\varepsilon_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds+C\int_{0}^{t}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+C(\kappa)\int_{0}^{t}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}(c_{k}(s),g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))dW_{2}(s))_{H_{2}^{1}}.

There exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that
\left|c_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}\leq C_{0}(\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}+\left|\Delta c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}})
. Therefore taking
\varepsilon_{1}=C_{1}:=\frac{\min(\alpha,r_{c})}{3}
in the previous inequality, we derive that

\displaystyle\left|c_{k}(t)\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\left|c_{0}\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C\int_{0}^{t}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\quad+C(\kappa)\int_{0}^{t}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds+2\int_{0}^{t}(c_{k}(s),g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))dW_{2}(s))_{H_{2}^{1}}.

Taking the supremum over
[0,T]
, the exponent
\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}
and the expectation, we arrive at

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|c_{k}(t)\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{1}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left|c_{0}\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C(m^{*},r^{*})\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C(\kappa,m^{*},r^{*})\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

(3.32)

\displaystyle\quad+C(m^{*},r^{*})\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{t}(c_{k}(s),g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))dW_{2}(s))_{H_{2}^{1}}\right|^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}.

We note that since
q>3
then
\frac{4}{q+1}<1
and therefore we obtain by the Hölder inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}(q+1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{4}{q+1}}.

Since
\xi\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}^{\kappa}
, we get

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq C(T)R^{\frac{4}{q+1}}.

By the Hölder inequality we obtain

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq C(T)\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds.

(3.33)

By the Burkholder-Gundy-Davis inequality, we have

\displaystyle C(m^{*},r^{*})\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{t}(c_{k}(s),g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))dW_{2}(s))_{H_{2}^{1}}\right|^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1};H_{2}^{1})}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{1}_{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1};H_{2}^{1})}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{1}_{2}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1};H_{2}^{1})}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}.

By using the definition of
g_{\gamma_{2}}
, we note that

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1};H_{2}^{1})}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}(\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{4}}+\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{4}})ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}(C\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}+\frac{C_{1}}{(2C)^{\frac{r^{*}}{2m^{*}}}}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}})ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+\frac{C_{1}^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds+\frac{C_{1}^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}},

and therefore

\displaystyle C(m^{*},r^{*})\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{t}(c_{k}(s),g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}(s))dW_{2}(s))_{H_{2}^{1}}\right|^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{1}_{2}}+\frac{C_{1}^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds.

Combining these last inequalities we infer from the inequality (
3.32
) that

\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|c_{k}(t)\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{1}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq\mathbb{E}\left|c_{0}\right|^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}}+C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds,

and the Gronwall lemma implies (
3.27
).

Finally, we consider the system

\displaystyle d{n}_{\kappa}(t)=(r_{n}\Delta|n_{\kappa}(t)|^{q-1}n_{\kappa}(t)+\theta\xi(t)-\chi\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)))dt+\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla\xi(t)+g_{\gamma_{3}}(n_{\kappa}(t))dW_{1}(t).

(3.34)

We now show that there exists a
unique solution
n_{\kappa}
to the system (
3.34
) by
verifying the assumptions (H1), (H2
′
), (H3), and (H4
′
) of Theorem 5.1.3 in
[
23
, p. 125]
. Let us consider the Gelfand triple

V\subset{{\mathcal{H}}}\cong{{\mathcal{H}}}^{\ast}\subset V^{\ast},

(3.35)

with
{{\mathcal{H}}}:=H_{2}^{-1}({{\mathcal{O}}})
,
V:=L^{q+1}({{\mathcal{O}}})
and
V^{\ast}=L^{\frac{{q+1}}{q}}({{\mathcal{O}}})
.
The duality
{}_{V^{\ast}}\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{V}
is defined as

{}_{V^{\ast}}\langle n,w\rangle_{V}=\int_{{{\mathcal{O}}}}((-\Delta)^{-1}n)(x)\,w(x)\,dx.

(3.36)

We set for
t\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle A(t,n):=r_{n}\Delta n^{[q]}+\theta\xi(t)-\chi\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t))+\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla\xi(t),

where
c_{\kappa}
solves (
3.30
) and
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}
solves (
3.29
).

First let us consider (H1).
Let
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}
. We need to show that

\lambda\mapsto{{}_{V^{\ast}}\langle A(t,n_{1}+\lambda n_{2}),w\rangle_{V}}

is continuous on
{\mathbb{R}}
, for any
n_{1},n_{2},w\in V
and
t\in[0,T]
.
As the map
\lambda\mapsto(n_{1}+\lambda n_{2})^{[q]}
is continuous, it follows by the dominated convergence Theorem
that the mapping
{\mathbb{R}}_{0}^{+}\ni\lambda\mapsto\int_{{{\mathcal{O}}}}(n_{1}+\lambda n_{2})^{[q]}wdx

is continuous. Hence, the mapping
\lambda\mapsto{{}_{V^{\ast}}\langle A(t,n_{1}+\lambda n_{2}),w\rangle_{V}}
is also continuous.

To show the local monotonicity, i.e. (H2
′
),
let us fix
n_{1},n_{2}\in V
and
t\in[0,T]
. Then, using the fact that (see
[
23
, Page 87-88]
),

-\int_{{{\mathcal{O}}}}\left[r_{n}(n_{1}^{[q]}-n_{2}^{[q]})(n_{1}-n_{2})\right]\,dx\leq 0,

we have

\displaystyle\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}\langle A(t,n_{1})-A(t,n_{2}),n_{1}-n_{2}\rangle_{V}}+|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{1})-g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{2})|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1},{{\mathcal{H}}})}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{{\mathcal{O}}}}\left[r_{n}(n_{1}^{[q]}-n_{2}^{[q]})(n_{1}-n_{2})\right]\,dx+|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{1})-g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{2})|^{2}_{{{\mathcal{L}}}_{2}(H_{1},{{\mathcal{H}}})}

\displaystyle\leq|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{1})-g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{2})|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1},{{\mathcal{H}}})}

(3.37)

\displaystyle\leq C|n_{1}-n_{2}|_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(f(t)+C)|n_{1}-n_{2}|_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}^{2},

where
f(t)
will be given later.

To verify the coercivity, i.e. (H3)
let us fix
n\in V
and
t\in[0,T]
. Then,

\displaystyle 2\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}\langle A(t,n),n\rangle_{V}}+|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n)|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1},{{\mathcal{H}}})}

\displaystyle=-2r_{n}|n|_{L^{q+1}}^{q+1}-2\chi\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)),n\rangle_{V}+2\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\nabla\xi(t)\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t),n\rangle_{V}

\displaystyle\quad+2\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\theta\xi(t),n\rangle_{V}+|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n)|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1},{{\mathcal{H}}})}

\displaystyle\leq-2r_{n}|n|_{V}^{q+1}+C(\theta)|n|_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}^{2}+|\xi|_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}^{2}+2\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\nabla\xi(t)\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t),n\rangle_{V}

\displaystyle\quad-2\chi\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)),n\rangle_{V}.

Since
q>3
then by the embedding of
V
into
{{\mathcal{H}}}
and the Young inequality we have

\displaystyle C(\theta)\left|n\right|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\theta)\left|n\right|^{2}_{V}\leq r_{n}\left|n\right|^{q+1}_{V}+C(\theta,q),

(3.38)

and

\displaystyle\left|\xi(t)\right|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\xi(t)\right|^{2}_{V}\leq\frac{1}{3}\left|\xi(t)\right|^{q+1}_{V}+C(q).

(3.39)

Similarly, since
q>3
, by the Hölder inequality and Sobolev embedding, we note that

\displaystyle-2\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\chi\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)),n\rangle_{V}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t))\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\xi(t)\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{L^{4}}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\xi(t)\right|_{L^{q+1}}^{2}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{q+1}}+\frac{1}{2}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}

(3.40)

\displaystyle\leq\left|\xi(t)\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+C\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\frac{1}{2}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}\left|\xi(t)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}+C\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+\frac{1}{2}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}+C(q).

By an integration by parts, we note that for all
w\in H^{1}_{2}(\mathcal{O})
,

\mathbin{{}_{H^{-1}_{2}}}\langle\xi(t)\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t),w\rangle_{H^{1}_{2}}=\int_{\mathcal{O}}\nabla\xi(t)\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)wdx=-\int_{\mathcal{O}}\xi(t)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\cdot\nabla wdx\leq\left|\xi(t)\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{\mathbf{L}^{4}}\left|\nabla w\right|_{L^{2}}.

This implies that
\left|\xi(t)\cdot\nabla\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\leq\left|\xi(t)\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}
and therefore,

\displaystyle 2\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\nabla\xi(t)\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t),n\rangle_{V}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\nabla\xi(t)\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}\left|\xi(t)\right|_{L^{4}}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{2}\left|\xi(t)\right|^{2}_{L^{q+1}}+\frac{1}{2}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}

(3.41)

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\xi(t)\right|^{4}_{L^{q+1}}+\frac{1}{2}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}+C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3}\left|\xi(t)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}+\frac{1}{2}\left|n\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}+C(q)+C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4}.

By using (
2
) and (
3.41
) we derive that

2\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}\langle A(t,n),n\rangle_{V}}+|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n)|^{2}_{L_{HS}(H_{1},{{\mathcal{H}}})}\leq C_{0}|n|_{{{\mathcal{H}}}}^{2}-r_{n}|n|_{V}^{\alpha^{*}}+f(t),

(3.42)

where
\alpha^{*}=q+1
,
C_{0}=\max(1,C(q)r_{n}^{\frac{q+1}{q}})
and

f(t)=\left|\xi(t)\right|^{4}_{L^{q+1}}+C\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4}+C(\theta,q).

Note that, since
\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}>1
, due to the estimates (
3.27
) and (
3.26
), there exists a constant
C(R)>0
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{4}_{H^{1}_{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{1}_{2}}\right)^{\frac{r^{*}}{4m^{*}}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{r^{*}}{4m^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(R).

By the Hölder inequality, we obtain

\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{4}_{{\bf H}}ds\leq T\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{4}_{{\bf H}}\leq T\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{8^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}}_{{\bf H}}\right)^{\frac{r^{*}}{2m^{*}}}\leq C(R).

Observe, due to the fact that
\xi\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}^{\kappa}
, it follows that
f
belongs to
L^{1}([0,T]\times\Omega;dt\otimes{\mathbb{P}})
.
This proves (H3).

The growth condition, i.e. (H4
′
), can be verified similarly to the boundedness.
Let
n\in V
and
t\in[0,T]
. Then for all
w\in V
, by the Hölder inequality we have

\displaystyle\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}\langle A(t,n),w\rangle_{V}}

\displaystyle\leq r_{n}\left|n\right|^{q}_{V}\left|w\right|_{V}+\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\theta\xi(t),w\rangle_{V}-\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\chi\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)),w\rangle_{V}

\displaystyle\quad+\mathbin{{}_{V^{\ast}}}\langle\nabla\xi(t)\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t),w\rangle_{V}

\displaystyle\leq r_{n}\left|n\right|^{q}_{V}\left|w\right|_{V}+\theta\left|\xi(t)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|w\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}+\left|\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t))\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|w\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\left|\nabla\xi(t)\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|w\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}.

Using the continuous embedding of
V
into
H^{-1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
we infer that

\displaystyle\left|A(t,n)\right|_{V^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq r_{n}\left|n\right|^{q}_{V}+C\theta\left|\xi(t)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}+C\left|\mbox{div}(\xi(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}(t))\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}+C\left|\nabla\xi(t)\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq r_{n}\left|n\right|^{q}_{V}+C\theta\left|\xi(t)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}+C\left|\xi(t)\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{L^{4}}+C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}\left|\xi(t)\right|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq r_{n}\left|n\right|^{q}_{V}+C\theta\left|\xi(t)\right|_{V}+C\left|\xi(t)\right|_{L^{q+1}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{L^{4}}+C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}\left|\xi(t)\right|_{L^{q+1}}.

Since
\frac{q+1}{q-1}<4
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\left|A(t,n)\right|^{\frac{\alpha^{*}}{\alpha^{*}-1}}_{V^{\star}}

\displaystyle\leq C(q)r^{\frac{q+1}{q}}_{n}\left|n\right|^{q+1}_{V}+C(\theta,q)\left|\xi(t)\right|^{\frac{q+1}{q}}_{V}+C(q)\left|\xi(t)\right|^{\frac{q+1}{q}}_{L^{q+1}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{\frac{q+1}{q}}_{L^{4}}+C(q)\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{\frac{q+1}{q}}\left|\xi(t)\right|^{\frac{q+1}{q}}_{L^{q+1}}

\displaystyle\leq C(q)r^{\frac{q+1}{q}}_{n}\left|n\right|^{q+1}_{V}+\left|\xi(t)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}+C(q)\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{\frac{q+1}{q-1}}_{L^{4}}+C(q)\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{\frac{q+1}{q-1}}+C(\theta,q)

\displaystyle\leq C(q)r^{\frac{q+1}{q}}_{n}\left|n\right|^{q+1}_{V}+\left|\xi(t)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}+C\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{4}_{L^{4}}+C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(t)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4}+C(\theta,q)

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\left|n\right|^{\alpha^{*}}_{V}+f(t)

\displaystyle\leq(C_{0}\left|n\right|^{\alpha^{*}}_{V}+f(t))(1+\left|n\right|^{2}_{{\mathcal{H}}}).

Observe, as before,
that
f\in L^{1}([0,T]\times\Omega;dt\otimes{\mathbb{P}})
.

In this way, we have proved that the Hypothesis (H1), (H2
′
), (H3), and (H4
′
) are satisfied.
The existence and uniqueness of
n_{\kappa}
such that for all
p\geq 2
,

\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{p}<\infty,

follows by an application of Theorem 5.1.3 in
[
23
, p.125]
.

To calculate the bound,
we apply the Itô formula to the function
\phi(n_{\kappa})=|n_{\kappa}|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}
and obtain

\displaystyle|n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}+2r_{n}\int_{0}^{t}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds

\displaystyle=|n_{0}|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}+2\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla^{-1}\xi(s),\nabla^{-1}n_{\kappa}(s))ds

\displaystyle\quad-2\chi\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla^{-1}n_{\kappa}(s),\nabla^{-1}(\mbox{div}(\xi(s)\nabla c_{\kappa}(s))))ds-2\int_{0}^{t}(\nabla^{-1}n_{\kappa}(s),\nabla^{-1}\left({\bf u}_{\kappa}(s)\cdot\nabla\xi(s)\right))ds

\displaystyle\quad{}+2\int_{0}^{t}\left(n_{\kappa}(s),g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}(s))\,dW_{1}(s)\right)_{H^{-1}_{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}(s))|_{L_{\text{HS}}(H_{1},H_{2}^{-1})}^{2}\,ds,

where
\nabla^{-1}:=-(-\Delta)^{-1/2}
.
Using the the fact that
L^{q+1}({{\mathcal{O}}})\hookrightarrow H^{-1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
we obtain

\displaystyle(\nabla^{-1}\xi,\nabla^{-1}n_{\kappa})\leq\left|\xi\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\leq C\left|\xi\right|_{L^{q+1}}\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|\xi\right|^{2}_{L^{q+1}}+C\left|n_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}.

By the Young inequality, we have

\displaystyle 2\chi(\nabla^{-1}n_{\kappa},\nabla^{-1}(\mbox{div}(\xi\nabla c_{\kappa})))

\displaystyle\leq C\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|\mbox{div}(\xi\nabla c_{\kappa})\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\xi\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}\right|_{L^{4}}\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|\xi\right|_{L^{4}}^{2}+C\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}\right|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|\xi\right|_{L^{q+1}}^{2}+C\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}\right|_{H_{2}^{2}}^{2}

and

\displaystyle 2(\nabla^{-1}n_{\kappa},\nabla^{-1}\left({\bf u}_{\kappa}\cdot\nabla\xi\right))

\displaystyle\leq 2\left|\nabla\xi\cdot\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|_{{\bf H}}\left|\xi\right|_{L^{4}}\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\leq\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|_{{\bf H}}^{2}\left|\xi\right|^{2}_{L^{q+1}}+C\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left|\xi\right|^{2}_{L^{q+1}}+\frac{1}{2}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4}+C\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}.

Using the inequality
3.17
, we obtain

|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa})|_{L_{\text{HS}}(H_{1},H_{2}^{-1})}^{2}\leq C\left|n_{\kappa}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}.

Combining these inequalities, we derive that

\displaystyle|n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}+2r_{n}\int_{0}^{t}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds

\displaystyle\leq|n_{0}|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{q+1}}ds+C\int_{0}^{t}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}ds+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4}ds

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{t}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|\xi(s)\right|_{L^{q+1}}^{2}ds+C\int_{0}^{t}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H_{2}^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\quad+2\int_{0}^{t}\left(n_{\kappa}(s),g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}(s))\,dW_{1}(s)\right)_{H^{-1}_{2}}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+(2r_{n})^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}|n_{0}|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|\xi(s)\right|_{L^{q+1}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

(3.43)

\displaystyle\quad+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H_{2}^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\left(n_{\kappa}(s),g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}(s))\,dW_{1}(s)\right)_{H^{-1}_{2}}\right|^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}.

We remark that we get by estimate (
3.26
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}}+C.

We note that a two times application of the Hölder inequality gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}(q+1)}}

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{2}{q+1}}

\displaystyle\leq CR^{\frac{2}{q+1}},

and

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq C(T,m^{*},r^{*})\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}ds,

as well as

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|\xi(s)\right|_{L^{q+1}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|_{L^{q+1}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|_{L^{q+1}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|_{L^{q+1}}^{q+1}ds\right)^{\frac{8m^{*}}{r^{*}(q+1)}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\left(\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi(s)\right|_{L^{q+1}}^{q+1}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{4}{q+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}}.

In a similar way we get

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{2}_{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{2}_{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H_{2}^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}.

Using the estimate (
3.27
), we derive that

\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{2}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left|c_{0}\right|^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}}.

By the Burkholder-Gundy-Davis inequality and the inequality
3.17
, we have

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|\int_{0}^{t}(n_{\kappa}(s),g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}(s))dW_{1}(s))_{H_{2}^{-1}}\right|^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{\text{HS}}(H_{1},H_{2}^{-1})}ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|n_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{-1}_{2}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{\text{HS}}(H_{1},H_{2}^{-1})}ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{2r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|n_{\kappa}(T)\right|^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{-1}_{2}}+C\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{\text{HS}}(H_{1},H_{2}^{-1})}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{6}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left|n_{\kappa}(t)\right|^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H^{-1}_{2}}+C(T)\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}ds.

From these a priori estimates, we infer from (
3.43
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\ |n_{\kappa}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+(2r_{n})^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}|n_{0}|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+C\mathbb{E}\left|\mathbf{u}_{0}\right|^{8\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\bf H}+C\mathbb{E}\left|c_{0}\right|^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C+CR^{\frac{2}{q+1}}+CR^{\frac{4}{q+1}}+C\mathbb{E}\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}(s)\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{4m^{*}}{r^{*}}}ds.

The Gronwall lemma implies (
3.28
) and ends the proof of Claim
3.6
.
∎

Verification of condition (
c
) of Theorem
2.2
:

Now, we shall tackle the continuity of the operator
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathfrak{A}}^{\kappa}
in the norm induced by
{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})
. In fact, in the next proposition we show that assumption (
c
) of Theorem
2.2
is satisfied.

Proposition 3.7
.

Let us assume that the assumptions of Theorem
3.4
are valid.
Let
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}(R)\subset{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})
be arbitrary. Then the following statements are valid.

(i)

Let
n_{\kappa}^{(1)}
and
n_{\kappa}^{(2)}
be the solutions to system

\displaystyle d{n}^{(j)}_{\kappa}(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(r_{n}\Delta|n^{(j)}_{\kappa}(t)|^{q-1}n^{(j)}_{\kappa}(t)+\theta\xi(t)-\chi\mbox{div}(\xi_{j}(t)\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(j)}(t))\right)\,dt

\displaystyle{}+\nabla\xi_{j}(t)\mathbf{u}^{(j)}_{\kappa}(t)dt+g_{\gamma_{1}}(n_{\kappa}^{(j)}(s))dW_{1},\phantom{\Big|}

with
n_{\kappa}^{(j)}(0)=n_{0},j=1,2
. Then,
for any
R>0
, for any
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
,
there exist constants
C=C(R,\kappa)>0
such that for any
\xi\in{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})\cap\mathcal{X}(R)
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}|n_{\kappa}^{(1)}(t)-n_{\kappa}^{(2)}(t)|_{H^{-1}_{2}}^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|n_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-n_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|^{q+1}_{L^{q+1}}ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbb{E}\|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{m^{*}}\right)^{\frac{2}{r^{*}}}+C(R)\left(\mathbb{E}\left\|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right\|_{\mathbb{X}}^{m^{*}}\right)^{\frac{r^{\prime}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+C(R)\ \left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{r^{\prime}}{4(q+1)}}+C(R)\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq T}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{r^{\prime}}{4q+4}}.

(ii)

Let
c_{\kappa}^{(1)}
and
c_{\kappa}^{(2)}
be the solutions to system

d{c_{\kappa}}^{(j)}(t)+\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(j)},t)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(j)}(t)\nabla c^{(j)}(t)\,dt=\left(r_{c}\Delta c_{\kappa}^{(j)}(t)-\alpha c_{\kappa}^{(j)}(t)+\beta\xi_{j}(t)\right)\,dt+g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}^{(j)}(s))dW_{2},

where
j=1,2
,
and
c_{\kappa}^{(j)}(0)=c_{0},j=1,2
. Then,
for any
R>0
, for any
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
,
there exist constants
C=C(R,\kappa)
such that for any
\xi\in{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})\cap\mathcal{X}(R)

we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|{c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}}(s)\right|^{\frac{2m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{L^{2}}+\alpha_{0}^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\left|{c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\mathbb{E}\left\|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right\|^{m^{*}}_{\mathbb{X}}\right)^{\frac{2}{r^{*}}}+C(\kappa,R)\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(iii)

Let
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}
and
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}
be the solutions to system

\displaystyle d\mathbf{u}^{(j)}_{\kappa}(t)-\Delta\mathbf{u}^{(j)}_{\kappa}(t)dt=\xi_{j}(t)\star\Phi\,dt+\sigma_{3}(t)dW_{3}(t),\phantom{\Big|}

with
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(j)}(0)=\mathbf{u}_{0},j=1,2
. Then,
for any
R>0
, for any
\kappa\in\mathbb{N}
,
there exist constants
C=C(R,\kappa)>0
such that for any
\xi\in{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})\cap\mathcal{X}(R)
,
we have

\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)|_{{\bf H}}^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+(2r)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{u}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)|_{{\bf V}}^{2}\,ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq C(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\left(\mathbb{E}\left\|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right\|^{m^{*}}_{\mathbb{X}}\right)^{\frac{2}{r^{*}}}.

The continuity of the operator
{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathfrak{A}}^{\kappa}:\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}(R)\to\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}(R)
follows by an interpolation argument.

Corollary 3.8
.

Let us assume that the assumptions of Theorem
3.4
are valid.
Let
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{A}}(R)\subset{{\mathcal{M}}}^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathfrak{A}}(\mathbb{X})
be arbitrary.
Then there exists some constant
C>0
such that

\mathbb{E}\left\|{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathfrak{A}}^{\kappa}(\xi_{1})-{{\mathcal{V}}}_{\mathfrak{A}}^{\kappa}(\xi_{2})\right\|^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathbb{X}}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left\|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right\|^{m^{\ast}}_{\mathbb{X}}.

Proof.

The proof is a combination of points (i), (ii) and (iii) of Proposition
3.7
with an application of Proposition
B.1
. We have to verify if the parameter
r=r^{\ast}
,
s=s^{\ast\ast}
, and
m=m^{\ast}
,
satisfy the constrains

r\in(2,q+1)
,
m\in(q+1,\infty)
,
s\in(0,1)
, with
\frac{1}{r}\geq\frac{1}{m}+\frac{s}{2}
. However, since
q>4
,
s^{**}=\frac{2}{q+1}
,
and the numbers
r^{\ast}
and
m^{\ast}
satisfy

q\leq r^{*}<q+1
and
m^{*}\geq 2q+2
, the constrains are satisfied and we can apply Proposition
B.1
.

∎

Proof of Proposition
3.7
.

Firstly, we consider the difference
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}
which is the solution of the following random equation

d(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)})(t)-\Delta(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)})(t)dt=(\xi_{1}(t)-\xi_{2}(t))\star\Phi dt,\ \text{ with }\ (\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)})(0)=0.

Testing this equation by
-\Delta(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)})
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)|_{{\bf H}}^{2}+2r_{u}\int_{0}^{t}|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)|_{{\bf V}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle=-2\int_{0}^{T}\left(\Delta(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)),(\xi_{1}(s)-\xi_{2}(s))\star\Phi\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)|_{{\bf H}}^{2}+C\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{0}^{T}|\nabla((\xi_{1}(s)-\xi_{2}(s))\star\phi_{i})|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}^{2}ds.

This implies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)|_{L^{2}}^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+(2r)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{u}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)|_{H^{1}_{2}}^{2}\,ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\nabla((\xi_{1}(s)-\xi_{2}(s))\star\phi_{i})|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}.

(3.44)

From semi-group theory (see, e.g.
[
25
, Chap. I]
), we obtain that for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle|\nabla((\xi_{1}-\xi_{2})\star\phi_{i})|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}|(\xi_{1}-\xi_{2})\star\phi_{i}|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|e^{-\delta_{i}\Delta}(\xi_{1}-\xi_{2})\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|e^{-\delta_{i}\Delta}\right|_{L(H^{-1}_{2};H^{1}_{2})}^{2}\left|(\xi_{1}-\xi_{2})\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|(\xi_{1}-\xi_{2})\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left|-\Delta e^{-\delta_{i}\Delta}\right|^{2}_{L(H^{-1}_{2};H^{-1}_{2})}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|(\xi_{1}-\xi_{2})\right|^{2}_{H^{-1}_{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{C}{\delta_{i}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{i})\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|_{H^{-1}_{2}}.

Using the embedding of
H^{-s^{**}}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
into
H^{-1}_{2}({{\mathcal{O}}})
, we infer that for
i=1,2
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\nabla((\xi_{1}(s)-\xi_{2}(s))\star\phi_{i})|_{\mathbf{L}^{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\delta_{i})\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|(\xi_{1}(s)-\xi_{2}(s))|_{H^{-s^{**}}_{2}}^{2}ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle=C(\delta_{i})\mathbb{E}\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{2}^{-s^{**}})}.

Since
m^{*}>2
and
2<r^{*}
we have

\mathbb{E}\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{L^{2}(0,T;H_{2}^{-s^{**}})}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{L^{m^{*}}(0,T;H_{r^{*}}^{-s^{**}})}\leq C\mathbb{E}\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{\mathbb{X}}\leq C\left(\mathbb{E}\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|^{m^{*}}_{\mathbb{X}}\right)^{\frac{2}{r^{*}}}.

We then infer from the inequality (
3.44
) that

\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)|_{{\bf H}}^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}+(2r)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}_{u}\mathbb{E}\left(\int_{0}^{T}|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)|_{{\bf V}}^{2}\,ds\right)^{\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq C(\delta_{1},\delta_{2})\left(\mathbb{E}\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|^{m^{*}}_{\mathbb{X}}\right)^{\frac{2}{r^{*}}}.

and the estimate for the difference
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}
follows.

Secondly, we consider the difference
c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}
which satisfies the following equation

\displaystyle d({c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}})(t)+\left(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)},t)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(t)\nabla c^{(1)}(t)-\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)},t)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(t)\nabla c^{(2)}(t)\right)dt

\displaystyle=\left(r_{c}\Delta(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)})(t)-\alpha(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)})(t)+\beta\xi_{1}(t)-\xi_{2}(t)\right)\,dt+g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(t)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(t))dW^{t}_{2}.

Setting
\alpha_{0}=\min(r_{c},\alpha)
and applying the Itô formula we arrive at

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|{c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+2\alpha_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\left|{c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{0}^{T}(\xi_{1}(s)-\xi_{2}(s),c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s))ds

\displaystyle\qquad+2\int_{0}^{T}\left(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)},s)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)},s)\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\nabla c^{(2)}(s),c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right)ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T}\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{\text{HS}}(H_{1},L^{2})}ds+2\sup_{0\leq t\leq{T}}\int_{0}^{t}(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s),g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s))dW_{2}(s)).

Since
s^{**}<1
, we have

\displaystyle(\xi_{1}-\xi_{2},c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)})

\displaystyle\leq\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|_{H_{2}^{-s^{**}}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|_{H_{2}^{s^{**}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|_{H_{2}^{-s^{**}}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|_{H_{2}^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\alpha_{0}}{3}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|^{2}_{H_{2}^{1}}+C\left|\xi_{1}-\xi_{2}\right|_{H_{2}^{-s^{**}}}^{2}.

For the second integrand, we have

\displaystyle\left(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)})\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)})\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\cdot\nabla c^{(2)},c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)})(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)})\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)},c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right)+\left(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)})\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\cdot\nabla(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}),c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\left((\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)})-\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}))\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)},c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right).

We note that

\displaystyle\left(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)})(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)})\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)},c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)})\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|_{\bf H}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{H^{1}_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|_{{\bf H}}^{2}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|_{L^{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)},s)(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s))\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s),c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq C(T)\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{2}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(3.45)

\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{3}\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds.

By an integration-by-part and the free divergence property of
\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}
, we obtain

\left(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)})\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\cdot\nabla(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}),c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right)=\frac{1}{2}\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)})\left(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\cdot\nabla(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)})^{2},1\right)=0.

For the control of the term
\left((\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)})-\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}))\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)},c_{\kappa}^{(1)}-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right)
, we introduce the stopping times
\tau_{i}
,
i=1,2
defined by for
i=1,2
,

\tau_{i}=\inf\left\{t\in[0,T]:\ \sup_{0\leq s\leq{t}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(i)}(s)\right|_{\bf H}\geq 2\kappa\right\},

and
\tau_{i}=T
if the set on the right hand side above is empty.

Without loss of generality we may assume that

\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2},\ \ \mathbb{P}\text{-a.s.}

and therefore, since for
i=1,2

\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(i)},t)=0\ \ \ \text{for}\ \ t\geq\tau_{2},

and
\Theta_{\kappa}(\cdot,t)
is Lipschitz with Lipschitz constant
\kappa^{-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left((\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)},s)-\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)},s))\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s),c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T}\left|(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)},s)-\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)},s))\right|\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau_{2}}\left|(\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)},s)-\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)},s))\right|\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{4}}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\kappa^{-2}\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau_{2}}\left|\sup_{0\leq s\leq{t}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)\right|_{\bf H}-\sup_{0\leq s\leq{t}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{\bf H}\right|^{2}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|^{2}_{{\bf H}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau_{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq C(\kappa)\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T\wedge\tau_{2}}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)\right|_{\bf H}^{2}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T\wedge\tau_{2}}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau_{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{H^{1}_{2}}ds.

In the last line, we have used the fact that for all
s\in[0,T\wedge\tau_{2})
,

\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T\wedge\tau_{2}}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}\right|_{\bf H}<2\kappa.

Using the fact that
T\wedge\tau_{2}\leq T
, we derive that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\left((\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)},s)-\Theta_{\kappa}(\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)},s))\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\cdot\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s),c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq C(\kappa)\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)\right|_{\bf H}^{2}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds+\frac{\alpha_{0}}{3}\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds.

We can easily see that

\int_{0}^{T}\left|g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s))\right|^{2}_{L_{\text{HS}}(H_{1},L^{2})}ds\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds.

From these last inequalities, we derive that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|{c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\alpha_{0}\int_{0}^{T}\left|{c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}}(s)\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\left|\xi_{1}(s)-\xi_{2}(s)\right|_{H_{2}^{-s^{**}}}^{2}ds+C\int_{0}^{T}\left|c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|^{2}_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\qquad+C(T)\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{2}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}

(3.46)

\displaystyle\qquad+C(\kappa)\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{2}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sup_{0\leq t\leq{T}}\left|\int_{0}^{t}(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s),g_{\gamma_{2}}(c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s))dW_{2}(s))\right|.

We note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(R)\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

and

\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\nabla c_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{L^{2}}^{2\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\leq C(R)\left(\mathbb{E}\sup_{0\leq s\leq{T}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(1)}(s)-\mathbf{u}_{\kappa}^{(2)}(s)\right|_{{\bf H}}^{4\frac{m^{*}}{r^{*}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.
